Thus gradient helper:
```
def grad_lse_weights(w,a):
 n=len(a); L=1 << (3*n-2 -1).bit_length()
 r = a[::-1]
 # ensure w length 2n-1
 W = np.fft.fft(w, L)
 R = np.fft.fft(r, L)
 g = np.fft.ifft(W*R).real
 return 2.0 * g[n-1:n-1+n]
```
But check orientation for w length: c_k = conv(a,a)_k for k=0..2n-2. Derivative g_i = 2∑_{k=0}^{2n-2} w_k p_{k-i} (where p out of range 0). r index n-1+j = p_{n-1-j}. conv(w,r)_{n-1+i} = ∑_k w_k r_{n-1+i-k} = p_{k-i}; yes. Good.

If using DFT P^2, gradient can also be computed with L=2n-1 and a different formula? But safe with L=3n.

```
def conv_self(a): n=len(a); L=1 << (2*n-2? size=2n-1 -1).bit_length(); A=fft(a,L); c=real(ifft(A*A)); return c[:2*n-1]
```

Compute LSE weights stably:
```
def lse_weights(c, beta):
  cm=c.max(); range=c.max()-c.min()+eps
  if range < eps: return uniform
  x=(c-cm)/range  # in [-1,0]
  # beta_eff = beta/range? Actually if x scaled by 1/range, exp(beta*x) has min e^{-beta*rng/range}=e^{-beta}; good.
  e=np.exp(beta*x)
  w=e/e.sum()
  return w
```
This corresponds to smoothing with parameter beta/range. Good. `beta` 10 to 500. If beta=10, weights spread; beta=500 top. Use increasing beta.

Gradient from weights: For LSE with scaled weights as above, the gradient of `F = cm + (1/beta_eff) logsumexp` is exactly ∑w ∂c, independent of beta_eff. So weights suffice. Great. We can ignore scalar in gradient; direction unaffected by scaling.

Now exact line search objective: `score(p)=2*n*max(conv(p,p))`. If using p sum1. We can return cmax directly and multiply 2n; for comparison use cmax.

Line search function to minimize score along p->q:
```
def line_search(p, q, score_p, step, min_step=1e-4, max_evals=6):
  # try step, step*2? maybe restrict [0,1]
  evals=0
  best_frac=0.0; best_val=score_p
  # candidate list: min(1, step), step/2, step/4, step/8, maybe 1.0
  # use current step as starting from previous accepted
  for frac in candidates:
    val = evaluate_score((1-frac)*p + frac*q)
    if val < best_val - 1e-15:
       best...
  if best_frac==0:
    # try much smaller
  return best_val, p_best, best_frac
```

    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)

Since coordinate local uses exact O(n) and no FFT for eval, can be fast. We can run many iterations on best.

Let's detail coordinate local optimizer:
```
def coordinate_local(p, max_time, rng, beta=100, max_iters=None, verbose=True):
    p = normalize(p)
    n=len(p); m=2*n-1
    c = conv_self(p)
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=score(c)
    it=0; no_improve=0; grad_interval=50
    step_frac_scale=1.0 # for sampling max fraction
    # Precompute fractions for candidate deltas
    fracs = np.array([1.0,0.7,0.5,0.35,0.25,0.15,0.1,0.05,0.03])
    # Actually if p_i small, full move okay.
    last_grad_time=0
    g=None
    while time<max_time:
       if it % grad_interval ==0 or g is None:
           w = lse_weights(c,beta)
           g = grad_from_weights(w,p)
           # center? use raw.
           top_i = np.argpartition(g, -20)[-20:]
           bot_j = np.argpartition(g, 20)[:20]
           beta = min(1000, beta*1.01) maybe
       propose pairs: sample a few high/low, also random if no_improve
       for each pair compute best move using exact sampling
       if improved:
           update p,c kept; best; no_improve=0; maybe beta dec? Iters.
       else:
           no_improve++
           if no_improve > threshold:
               perturb p: add small Gaussian and normalize, recompute c
               no_improve=0
               beta = max(20, beta*0.8)
    return best_p,best_score,c
```
Need assess coordinate update O(n) cost. If each move involves evaluating 9 candidates each with max O(m), that's 9*16k ~147k element ops. Python vectorization in loops 9 times; maybe ~0.5 ms? 10k moves ~5s. Great. We can do 100k moves ~50s. Good.

But computing top lists via FFT every 50 moves: 2k FFTs if 100k moves; okay. Could compute weights and gradient every 200 moves to reduce. But gradient must be current; 50 okay.

Could update c analytically but repeated small δ can accumulate error in c (max error ~1e-15 per op times moves). Recompute via FFT when error possible or every 2000 accepted moves. Since score target 1e-3, not critical. But for local precision, recompute periodically and when best improvement small.

Candidate δ selection: We sample fractions of max move p_i. But if objective decreases very slightly for δ small and increases later, full may not accepted. We can keep best among fractions. To avoid too small improvements causing noise, require improvement > 1e-12 maybe; but many tiny can accumulate. We can accept if score < best_score - 1e-12.

If no pair among top/bottom improves, try alternate directions (low to high?) Actually if g_i high, j low but maybe moving high->low not decrease due nonconvex/other peaks. The opposite direction might improve? It would increase surrogate gradient, unlikely. But due sampling maybe. We can try both directions for selected pairs: move i->j and j->i; choose best.

Pair selection with p_i: If high i has very small p, full move tiny; perhaps select top among g/p? We want relative change; coordinate descent with p_i. We can score benefit by `(g_i - g_j) * p_i`. Choose pairs maximizing. For high/low lists, compute score matrix? 20x20 =400, cheap. Choose top 5.

Let's define `evaluate_pair_move(p,c,i,j,fracs)`:
```
def evaluate_pair_move(p,c,i,j,fracs):
    # move Δ from i to j
    pi=p[i]

Let's design Bregman line search in detail, with overhead moderate.

Function `breman_step(p, beta=50, dir_beta=2, step=0.2, eval_limit=6)`:
1. c=conv(p), score.
2. w=lse_weights(c,beta); g=grad_from_weights(w,p)
3. Range g = g.max()-g.min(); if range<eps fail.
4. Build q: `scale = max(range, 1e-300); u = -dir_beta*(g - g.min())/scale; q = p*exp(u); q/=q.sum()`. Maybe clip p>0. If q has zeros due underflow, add small floor: q = np.maximum(q, 1e-30); normalize.
5. Directional derivative of optimal smooth approx negative. Evaluate line search exact score on pγ=(1-γ)p+γq for fractions: [step, step/2, step/4, step*2 capped, 1.0, step/8]. Choose best. This could be 5 conv. If no improvement, reduce dir_beta? Actually if q direction bad, maybe smaller dir_beta (q closer to p) or larger? If q very skewed and small step from p still bad due direction; try q with lower dir_beta. We can do a loop over dir_beta [1,2,5] maybe. But expensive.
6. Accept best if score < current. Update step: if best_frac > step*1.2 step*=1.2; if best_frac < step*0.8 step*=0.8.
Return new p, score.

For p sum1, q sum1. `pγ` sum1.

```
def grad_from_weights(w, a):
    n=len(a); m=2*n-1
    L=1 << (3*n-2 - 1).bit_length()
    r = a[::-1]
    W = fft(w, L)
    R = fft(r, L)
    g = ifft(W*R).real
    return 2.0 * g[n-1:n-1+n]
```
Check length: w length m. If L includes 3n-2. For n maybe 8192, L=32768. Good.
Could there be sign/offset wrong? Let's test with simple w0 at n=2: w=[1,0,0], a=[a,b], L=5? 3n-2=4, next pow2=4? Wait bit_length: (4-1=3).bit_length=2, L=4. Need L >= len(w)+len(r)-1 =4? w len3,r len2 => conv len4, okay. d index n-1=1. W* R. r=[b,a]. Linear conv w*r length4: [b,a,0,0], index1=a -> 2a. good. If L=4 circular conv length4 no wrap? Conv length4 exactly, circular length4 equals linear (last index 3), good. For n=3, w len5,r len3, conv len7, L=8. good.

Return g.
- `pair_update_exact(p,c,i,j,fracs)` as above. Returns (delta, new_ccopy_or_delta, new_score, d?) We don't want copy if not improve. We'll implement to test and if best_delta > threshold, apply to p,c in place and return score.
```
def apply_best_pair_move(p,c,i,j,fracs, improve_tol=1e-14):
   if i==j or p[i]<=0: return False
   m=len(c); n=len(p)
   d = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
   d[i:i+n] -= 2.0 * p
   d[j:j+n] += 2.0 * p
   best_delta=0; best_max=c.max(); best_val=best_max
   delta_sq? for candidates
   for frac in fracs:
      delta = p[i]*frac
      if delta <= 0: continue
      arr = c + delta*d
      # point adjustments
      ii=2*i
      if ii<m: arr[ii] += delta*delta
      jj=2*j
      if jj<m: arr[jj] += delta*delta
      ij=i+j
      if i != j and ij<m: arr[ij] -= 2.0*delta*delta
      val=arr.max()
      if val < best_max - tol:
          best_max=val; best_delta=delta
   if best_delta>0:
      p[i] -= best_delta
      p[j] += best_delta
      c += best_delta*d
      ii=2*i ... c[ii] += best_delta^2; c[jj]+=...; c[ij]-=...
      # fix tiny negative
      # c = np.maximum(c,0.0) maybe expensive; skip unless recompute
      return best_max
   return None
```
But if q point adjustment negative and candidate delta such that arr at cross index becomes negative due roundoff? Actual nonnegative; we can ignore. For score max negative not an issue. If c has small negative due accumulated error, max may ignore; but when apply repeated, c could drift. Recompute every N moves.

Let's detail `normalize_sum`:
```
def normalize_sum(a, s=1.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    a = np.clip(a,0.0,None)
    tot=a.sum()
    if tot <= 0: return np.full(len(a), s/len(a)) if len(a)>0 else np.array([1.0])
    a *= s/tot
    # optionally enforce finite
    return a
```
If length 0? Not.

`conv_self`:
```
def conv_self(a):
    n=len(a)
    size=2*n-1
    L=next_pow2(size)
    A=fft(a, L)
    c=ifft(A*A).real
    return c[:size]
```
Potential small negative c due numerical; can `np.maximum(c,0)` maybe. For score max no issue but for coordinate exact updates negative may accumulate. In convolution, set `c[c<0 & >-1e-12]=0`? `c = np.where(c>0, c, 0.0)` might hide errors but rounded okay. Use `np.maximum(c,0.0)`.

def sequence_score(a):
  n=len(a); c=conv_self(a); return float(2*n*c.max())

`lse_weights`:
```
def lse_weights(c,beta):
  cmax=c.max(); cmin=c.min()
  rng = cmax-cmin
  if rng <= 1e-300:
      m=len(c); return np.full(m,1.0/m)
  x=(c-cmax)/rng  # [-1,0]
  # cap beta to 700
  b=min(float(beta),700.0)
  e=np.exp(b*x)
  s=e.sum()
  if s<=0 or not np.isfinite(s):
      # fallback top1
      w=np.zeros_like(c); idx=np.argmax(c); w[idx]=1.0; return w
  return e/s
```
If beta large and x min -1, e min e^{-700} ~1e-304, okay. Underflow to 0 fine.

`grad_weights` using `fft` from numpy. Need import `fft` and `ifft` top-level. If using `np.fft.fft`, okay. We'll import for speed.
```
def grad_weights(w,a):
  n=len(a); L=next_pow2(3*n-2)
  r=a[::-1]
  W=fft(np.asarray(w)[:2*n-1], L) # ensure length
  R=fft(r, L)
  g=ifft(W*R).real
  return 2.0*g[n-1:n-1+n]
```
Need if `w` length maybe c length. Good.

`peak_gradient_single` maybe for selection? We can include:
```
def peak_gradient_single(p,k):
  n=len(p); g=np.zeros(n)
  lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
  if lo<=hi:
    g[lo:hi+1] = 2.0 * p[k-hi : k-lo+1][::-1]
  return g
```
Check k-hi for k=0: hi=0,k-hi=0,k-lo+1=1 slice p[0:1] ok. For k=2n-2: lo=n-1,hi=n-1, k-hi=n-1,k-lo+1=n slice p[n-1:n]. ok.

`pair_move` exact:
```
def pair_move(p,c,i,j,fracs, tol=1e-14):
    if i==j: return None
    pi=p[i]
    if pi <= 1e-300: return None
    n=len(p); m=len(c); base_max=float(c.max())
    d = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    # p shift
    d[i:i+n] -= 2.0 * p
    d[j:j+n] += 2.0 * p
    best_delta=0.0; best_val=base_max
    # deltas unique sorted maybe descending? If function improves at small and large, loop all.
    for frac in fracs:
        delta = pi * frac
        if delta <= 0.0: continue
        arr = c + delta * d
        ii = i + i
        if ii < m: arr[ii] += delta*delta
        jj = j + j
        if jj < m: arr[jj] += delta*delta
        ij = i + j
        if i != j and ij < m: arr[ij] -= 2.0 * delta*delta
        val = arr.max()
        if val < best_val - tol:
            best_val = float(val); best_delta = delta
    if best_delta > 0:
        p[i] -= best_delta
        p[j] += best_delta
        c += best_delta * d
        ii=i+i
        if ii<m: c[ii] += best_delta*best_delta
        jj=j+j
        if jj<m: c[jj] += best_delta*best_delta
        ij=i+j
        if i!=j and ij<m: c[ij] -= 2.0*best_delta*best_delta
        # guard tiny negatives
        # If we don't clip, future max unaffected but dot maybe; clip maybe small overhead.
        # c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
        return float(best_val)
    return None
```
Potential issue: For candidate delta, if q adjustment at same index (ii==jj or ij) order not additive if same index? We add separately; if ii==ij and i==j? not. If i!=j and 2i = i+j => i=j, no. 2j = i+j => i=j. So no. If ii==jj only i=j. Fine.
 `c += best_delta*d` modifies c in place. But `d` allocated. Good.
If best_val > base due no improvement returns None. If improvement tiny < tol, skip; maybe can accumulate too many tiny; use tol=0.0? To allow tiny, set tol=1e-15. But due float noise, accept >1e-12? We want improve score by 1e-4, so accept 1e-12. Use `tol=1e-12 * max(1.0, base_max)`? base_max ~1e-4, tol=1e-16. Use `1e-14` absolute? c ~1e-4, improvement maybe 1e-10. Use `tol=1e-14` might be too large relative? No 1e-14 small. Good.

But `pair_move` exact line: It computes d based on p before move. For candidate deltas, formula includes δ^2 adjustments. However for cross term if k=i+j, coefficient -2; if i/j intervals overlap? The d intervals plus q formula exact? Let's test with c0 and move from i=0 to j=1,n=2 p=[0.7,0.3]. m=3,c=[0.49,0.42,0.09]. d: d[0:2]-=2p => [-1.4,-0.6,0]; d[1:3]+=2p => [ -1.4, -0.6+1.4=0.8,0.6]. Move δ=0.1 from 0 to1: c+δd=[0.35,0.50,0.15]; q: i0 q0+0.01 =>0.36; j1 q2+0.01=>0.16; ij1 q1-2*0.01=>0.48. Actual p=[0.6,0.4], c=[0.36,0.48,0.16]. Good.

def coordinate_local(p, max_time, rng, label=""):
    start=time.monotonic()
    p=normalize_sum(p)
    c=conv_self(p); score=sequence_score_from_c(p,c)
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=score
    beta=30.0
    grad_age=10**9; recompute_age=10**9; no_improve=0
    fracs = PAIR_FRACS
    candidates=[]
    while time <= max_time:
       if len(candidates)==0 or grad_age > 50:
          # build candidates
          w=lse_weights(c,beta)
          g=grad_weights(w,p)
          topg = np.argpartition(g, -12)[-12:]
          botg = np.argpartition(g, 12)[:12]
          topg = topg[np.argsort(g[topg])[::-1]] maybe
          botg = botg[np.argsort(g[botg])]
          cand=[]
          for i in topg[:6]:
             if p[i] <= 1e-300: continue
             for j in botg[:6]:
                if i!=j: cand.append((i,j))
          # active peaks
          topc = np.argsort(c)[-5:]
          for k in topc[:3]:
             gp=peak_gradient_single(p, int(k))
             if gp.max() - gp.min() > 1e-300:
                topk = np.argpartition(gp, -8)[-8:]
                botk = np.argpartition(gp, 8)[:8]
                topk = topk[np.argsort(gp[topk])[::-1]]
                botk = botk[np.argsort(gp[botk])]
                for i in topk[:4]:
                   if p[i] <= 1e-300: continue
                   for j in botk[:4]:
                       if i != j: cand.append((i,j))
          # Remove duplicates via tuple set maybe simple list check
          unique=[]; seen=set()
          for i,j in cand:
             if i!=j and (i,j) not in seen and p[i]>0:
                seen.add((i,j)); unique.append((i,j))
          candidates=unique[:30]
          rng.shuffle(candidates)? rng.shuffle on list works. But shuffle each refresh.
          grad_age=0
       if recompute_age > 500:
          c=conv_self(p); recompute_age=0
          # maybe update best?
          score=sequence_score_from_c(p,c)
       progress=(time.monotonic()-start)/max_time
       # adapt beta higher as progress? but coordinate uses weights; beta maybe 50 to 300
       beta = min(400.0, 20.0 + 200.0*progress)
       improved=False
       for i,j in candidates:
          # optional if p[i] too small skip
          new_score = pair_move(p,c,i,j,fracs)
          if new_score is not None:
             score = new_score
             grad_age += 1; recompute_age +=1
             if score < best_score - 1e-13:
                 best_score=score; best_p=p.copy()
             improved=True
             # after a successful move, maybe break to try other candidates next loop? We can continue but state changed; remaining candidates old maybe still okay. To avoid too many O(n) per outer, break after one success.
             break
       if not improved:
          no_improve +=1
          if no_improve > 100:
              # perturb
              noise = 0.005 / np.sqrt(n) * (1.0 + 0.5*progress?) maybe
              p = p + rng.normal(0.0, noise, n)
              p = normalize_sum(p)
              c=conv_self(p)
              score=sequence_score_from_c(p,c)
              candidates=[]
              no_improve=0
              beta=max(10.0,beta*0.7)
              # If perturbed score worse than best, keep best but continue
       if score <= target*0.999999? Maybe continue but can set flag.
       grad_age += 1 if improved else len(candidates)+1? Simpler increment attempts: attempts counter.
```

Implement `breman_local` simpler:
```
def breman_local(p, max_time, rng, label=""):
   p=normalize_sum(p); c=conv; score; best...
   beta=30; dir_beta=0.5; step=0.1
   it=0
   while time<max:
      if it%5==0: c=conv; score
      w=lse_weights(c,beta); g=grad_weights(w,p)
      grange=g.max()-g.min()
      if grange < 1e-15: break
      u = -dir_beta * (g - g.min())/grange
      q = p * np.exp(u)
      q = normalize_sum(q)
      # candidates fractions
      best_frac=step
      # evaluate fractions: step, step/2, step/4, min(1,1.5*step),1.0? But c needs conv for each.
      base=score
      best_val=base; best_p=p.copy()
      for frac in [step, step*0.5, step*0.25, min(1.0, step*2.0), 1.0]:
         if frac<=0 or frac>1: continue
         p_try = (1-frac)*p + frac*q
         val = score_from_c(conv_self(p_try), n)
         if val < best_val - 1e-14: best...
      if best_val < base:
         p = best_p; # c not updated? set c=conv(p) maybe if not needed; score=best_val
         update step: if best_frac>step: step=min(1,step*1.2); elif best_frac<step: step=max(0.02,step*0.7)
         beta=min(300,beta*1.01)
         dir_beta=min(3.0,dir_beta*1.05) maybe
      else:
         # reduce direction or perturb q
         dir_beta *= 0.7
         if dir_beta < 0.1: dir_beta=2.0; beta=max(10,beta*0.8); p += small noise? 
      it+=1
   return best...
```
This may be slow due many conv. Use limited time.

Maybe no Bregman to reduce code complexity? But coordinate local from inverse sqrt might converge to local but okay. Starting given likely best. I'll include a lightweight Bregman for global but code manageable.

Potential issue: `np.argpartition` on 1D with kth integer; if n large and k out? We'll ensure k <= n-1. For high length 12, if n<12? n likely >128. But for small lengths maybe. We'll define helper `topk_args(values,k,which='max')`:
```
def topk_indices(values, k, ascending=False):
    n=len(values)
    k=min(k,n)
    if k<=0: return np.array([],dtype=int)
    if not ascending:
       part = np.argpartition(values, n-k)[n-k:]
       order = np.argsort(values[part])[::-1]
       return part[order]
    else:
       part = np.argpartition(values, k)[:k]
       order = np.argsort(values[part])
       return part[order]
```
This avoids watching kth. For botg, ascending=True.

def make_initials(n, rng, given=None):
   inits=[]
   if given is not None and len(given)>=32:
      if len(given)==n: inits.append(normalize_sum(given))
      else: inits.append(upscale(normalize_sum(given),n))
   idx=np.arange(n, dtype=float)
   # one-sided inverse sqrt
   for a in [0.3,0.7,1.5,3.0]:
      p=1/np.sqrt(idx+a)
      inits.append(normalize_sum(p))
      inits.append(normalize_sum(p[::-1]))
   # sum left+right
   for a in [0.7,1.5]:
      left=1/np.sqrt(idx+a); right=1/np.sqrt((n-1-idx)+a); inits.append(normalize_sum(left+right))
   # given powers? later
   # windows? maybe
   p=1+0.3*np.sin(2*np.pi*idx/n); inits.append(normalize_sum(np.maximum(p,0.1)))
   # uniform? maybe not
   return inits
```

`upscale`:
```
def upscale(a,n):
   m=len(a); if m==n: return a
   old=np.linspace(0,1,m); new=np.linspace(0,1,n); return normalize_sum(np.interp(new,old,a))
```

Let's write `coordinate_local` carefully:
```
def coordinate_local(p, max_time, rng, label="", target=1.5027):
    if max_time <= 0: return normalize_sum(p), sequence_score(p)
    n=len(p); p=normalize_sum(p)
    c=conv_self(p); score=2.0*n*c.max()
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=score
    fracs=PAIR_FRACS
    beta=30.0
    candidates=[]
    attempts_since_grad=10**9
    recompute_count=0
    since_success=0
    start=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic()-start < max_time:
       if since_success > 150 or attempts_since_grad > 40 or len(candidates)==0:
           # build candidates
           ...
           since_success=0; attempts_since_grad=0
       if recompute_count >= 300:
           c=conv_self(p); recompute_count=0; score=2.0*n*c.max()
           if score < best_score: update
       progress = min(1.0, (time.monotonic()-start)/max_time)
       beta = min(500.0, 20.0 + 480.0*progress)
       improved=False
       for (i,j) in candidates:
           if not (0 <= i < n and 0 <= j < n): continue
           if i==j or p[i] <= 1e-300: continue
           new_score = pair_move(p,c,i,j,fracs)
           attempts_since_grad += 1
           if new_score is not None:
               score = new_score
               recompute_count += 1
               if score < best_score - 1e-14:
                   best_score=score; best_p=p.copy()
               improved=True
               since_success=0
               # break after one success to refresh less often
               break
       if not improved:
           attempts_since_grad += len(candidates)
           since_success += 1
           if since_success > 200:
              # perturb
              noise = (0.002 + 0.004*progress)/np.sqrt(n)
              p = p + rng.normal(0.0, noise, n)
              p = normalize_sum(p)
              c = conv_self(p)
              score = 2.0*n*c.max()
              if score > best_score + 0.02:
                  p = best_p.copy(); c=conv_self(p); score=best_score
              candidates=[]
              since_success=0
              beta=max(10.0, beta*0.7)
       # if score very close target optionally continue
    # final exact score
    p=normalize_sum(p)
    c=conv_self(p); score=2.0*n*c.max()
    if score < best_score:
       best_score=score; best_p=p
    return best_p, float(best_score)
```

def power_search(p, gammas=(0.5,0.7,0.85,1.0,1.2,1.5,2.0)):
   best_p,p_score = p, score(p)
   for gamma...:
       q=power_transform(p,gamma); s=score(q); if s<best: ...
   return best_p,best_score
```

def shape_search(p, gammas, rev=False):
   best...
   candidates=[p]
   if rev: candidates.append(p[::-1])
   for q in candidates:

`next_pow2`: if m=0? size>0. Use:
```
def next_pow2(m):
    m = max(1, int(m))
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()
```

`topk_indices`:
```
def topk_indices(x, k, ascending=False):
    n = len(x)
    if k <= 0 or n == 0: return np.empty(0, dtype=int)
    k = min(k, n)
    if ascending:
        part = np.argpartition(x, k - 1)[:k]
        part = part[np.argsort(x[part])]
    else:
        part = np.argpartition(x, n - k)[n - k:]
        part = part[np.argsort(x[part])[::-1]]
    return part
```
For k=1, ascending partition k-1=0 okay. For negative inf? no.

`normalize_sum`:
If a length 0, return array([1.0]) maybe. But propose always nonempty. Use:
```
def normalize_sum(a, s=1.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if a.size == 0: return np.array([1.0])
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a,0.0,None)
    tot = float(a.sum())
    if tot <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(a.size, s / a.size)
    return a * (s / tot)
```

`conv_self`: handle small n. `if n==1: return np.array([a[0]*a[0]])` maybe. FFT fine.
```
def conv_self(a):
    n=len(a)
    if n==0: return np.array([0.0])
    size=2*n-1
    L=next_pow2(size)
    A=fft(a, L)
    c=ifft(A*A).real
    c[:size]=np.maximum(c[:size],0.0) # careful: c[:size] returns view? yes.
    return c[:size]
```
But `np.maximum(c[:size],0.0)` returns array; assign? Use `c = c[:size]; c = np.maximum(c,0.0); return c`.

`score_from_c`:
```
def score_from_c(c,n):
    if c.size==0: return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * c.max())
```

`sequence_score`: normalize? Do not normalize, just compute from raw with sum? Since score scale invariant but formula uses sum^2. If we always pass p sum1, score=2n max. For safety:
```
def sequence_score(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float).reshape(-1); a=np.clip(a,0,None); s=a.sum(); n=len(a); if s<0.01: return inf; c=conv_self(a); return 2*n*c.max()/s*s
```

`power_transform`:
```
def power_transform(p, gamma):
    p=np.maximum(np.asarray(p,float),1e-300)
    q=np.power(p,gamma)
    return normalize_sum(q)
```

`shape_search` uses `np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))`. For combinations, if p and rev. Implementation:
```
def shape_search(p, max_time=None, gammas=(0.6,0.8,1.0,1.25,1.6), mix_reverse=True):
   p=normalize_sum(p); n=len(p)
   best_p=p.copy(); best_s=score_from_c(conv_self(p),n)
   candidates=[p]
   if mix_reverse:
       rev=p[::-1].copy(); candidates.append(rev)
   # for each base, powers
   for base in candidates:
       for g in gammas:
           q=power_transform(base,g); s=...; update
   # log mixes (if two candidates)
   if mix_reverse:
       lp=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300)); lr=np.log(np.maximum(p[::-1],1e-300))
       for th in (0.3,0.5,0.7):
           q=normalize_sum(np.exp(th*lp+(1-th)*lr)); update
   return best_p,best_s
```
This does many conv; gammas 5*2 +3=13, plus base 2 =15 conv. For n=8192 okay (<0.5s). For n=32768 15 conv ~ maybe 1s. Good.

       p=normalize_sum(p); return p, sequence_score_sum1(p)
    n=len(p); p=normalize_sum(p)

    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=score

    success_streak? maybe no.

    since_success=0

    while True:
       elapsed=time.monotonic()-start
       if elapsed >= max_time: break
       if len(candidates)==0 or attempts_since_grad >= 25 or since_success > 250:
           # build candidates

Let's implement `pair_move` with `d` allocation. Need not check finite. If `d` all zeros? If p all zero? no.

```
def pair_move(p,c,i,j,fracs, tol=1e-14):
    n=len(p)
    pi=float(p[i])
    if i==j or pi <= 1e-300: return None
    m=len(c)
    base_max = float(c.max())
    d = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    d[i:i+n] -= 2.0 * p
    d[j:j+n] += 2.0 * p
    best_delta=0.0; best_val=base_max
    delta_sq? inside
    for frac in fracs:
       delta = pi * frac
       if delta >= pi: delta = pi*0.999999999
       if delta <= 0: continue
       arr = c + delta * d
       q2 = delta*delta
       ii = i+i
       if ii < m: arr[ii] += q2
       jj = j+j
       if jj < m: arr[jj] += q2
       ij = i+j
       if i != j and ij < m: arr[ij] -= 2.0*q2
       val = float(arr.max())
       if val < best_val - tol:
           best_val=val; best_delta=delta
    if best_delta > 0.0:
       p[i] = max(0.0, p[i] - best_delta)
       p[j] = p[j] + best_delta
       c += best_delta * d
       q2=best_delta*best_delta
       ii=i+i; if ii<m: c[ii]+=q2
       jj=j+j; if jj<m: c[jj]+=q2
       ij=i+j; if i!=j and ij<m: c[ij]-=2*q2
       # tiny negative? skip.
       return best_val
    return None
```
If p[i] very small and frac=1 -> delta=pi*0.999999999, p[i] left 1e-9*pi; okay. If we want exact zero possible, not needed.

Potential issue: If `d` contains p values and c is modified in place, when computing `c += best_delta*d`, c is also used in candidate `arr = c + delta*d` which creates new array; no alias. Good.

    except Exception:

`upscale_sequence`:
```
def upscale_sequence(a, n):
   a=normalize_sum(a)
   if len(a)==n: return a
   old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,len(a))
   new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
   return normalize_sum(np.interp(new, old, a))
```
If n=1? okay.

`make_initials`:
```
def make_initials(n, given=None):
   inits=[]
   if given is not None and len(given) >= 8:
       if len(given)==n: inits.append(normalize_sum(given))
       else: inits.append(upscale_sequence(given,n))
   idx=np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64)
   # inverse sqrt
   for a in (0.3,0.8,1.6,3.0):
       p=1.0/np.sqrt(idx+a)
       inits.append(normalize_sum(p))
       inits.append(normalize_sum(p[::-1]))
       q=1.0/np.sqrt(idx+a) + 1.0/np.sqrt((n-1-idx)+a)
       inits.append(normalize_sum(q))
   # sinusoidal windows
   t=idx/(n-1 if n>1 else 1)
   for amp in (0.25,0.5):
       p=1.0 + amp*np.sin(2*np.pi*t)
       inits.append(normalize_sum(np.maximum(p,0.1)))
   # randomish block? maybe use np.linspace? no rng? make_initials could accept rng for random.
   return inits
```
No rng needed. Maybe add random at main.

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step heights minimizing
    2 * n * max(convolve(a, a)) / (sum(a))^2.

The approach is local-improvement driven:
  - exact one-coordinate line moves with analytic convolution updates,
  - peak/LSE gradient-based candidate direction generation,
  - power reparameterization and reversible geometric mixing for basin changes,
  - short restarts / perturbations when stuck,
  - optional extension to a larger length (capped at 16384) before returning.
"""

import time
import numpy as np
from numpy.fft import fft, ifft

TARGET = 1.5027
MAX_RETURN_N = 16384

PAIR_FRACS = (1.0, 0.75, 0.5, 0.35, 0.2, 0.1, 0.05)


def next_pow2(m):
    m = max(1, int(m))
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def normalize_sum(a, s=1.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    tot = float(a.sum())
    if tot <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(a.size, s / a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return a * (s / tot)


def conv_self(a):
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([0.0], dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([float(a[0]) * float(a[0])], dtype=np.float64)
    size = 2 * n - 1
    L = next_pow2(size)
    A = fft(a, L)
    c = ifft(A * A).real
    c = c[:size]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def score_from_c(c, n):
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * c.max())


def sequence_score_sum1(a):
    a = normalize_sum(a)
    n = len(a)
    c = conv_self(a)
    return score_from_c(c, n)


def topk_indices(x, k, ascending=False):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    n = len(x)
    if n == 0 or k <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=int)
    k = min(k, n)
    if ascending:
        part = np.argpartition(x, k - 1)[:k]
        part = part[np.argsort(x[part])]
    else:
        part = np.argpartition(x, n - k)[n - k:]
        part = part[np.argsort(x[part])[::-1]]
    return part


def lse_weights(c, beta):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    cmax = float(c.max())
    cmin = float(c.min())
    rng = cmax - cmin
    if rng <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(c.size, 1.0 / c.size, dtype=np.float64)
    b = float(beta)
    if not np.isfinite(b) or b < 0.0:
        b = 50.0
    b = min(700.0, b)
    x = (c - cmax) / rng
    e = np.exp(b * x)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
        w = np.zeros_like(c, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return w
    return e / s


def grad_weights(w, a):
    """
    Gradient of sum_k w_k * conv(a,a)_k with respect to a.
    For c_k = sum_i a_i a_{k-i},
    d c_k / d a_i = 2 a_{k-i} (with a out of range = 0).
    """
    n = len(a)
    m = 2 * n - 1
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)[:m]
    L = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    r = a[::-1]
    W = fft(w, L)
    R = fft(r, L)
    g = ifft(W * R).real
    return 2.0 * g[n - 1:n - 1 + n]


def peak_gradient_single(p, k):
    """Gradient of the single convolution coefficient c_k wrt p."""
    n = len(p)
    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
    hi = min(n - 1, k)
    if lo <= hi:
        g[lo:hi + 1] = 2.0 * p[k - hi:k - lo + 1][::-1]
    return g


def pair_move(p, c, i, j, fracs, tol=1e-14):
    """
    Move mass from coordinate i to coordinate j:
        p_i -> p_i - delta
        p_j -> p_j + delta
    Exact updates of the convolution are used.
    Returns the new max coefficient if an improving delta is found, otherwise None.
    """
    i = int(i)
    j = int(j)
    if i == j:
        return None
    n = len(p)
    m = len(c)
    pi = float(p[i])
    if pi <= 1e-300:
        return None

    base_max = float(c.max())
    d = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)

    # delta * (2 p_{k-j} - 2 p_{k-i})
    d[i:i + n] -= 2.0 * p
    d[j:j + n] += 2.0 * p

    best_delta = 0.0
    best_val = base_max

    for frac in fracs:
        delta = pi * float(frac)
        if delta >= pi:
            delta = pi * 0.999999999
        if delta <= 0.0:
            continue

        arr = c + delta * d
        q2 = delta * delta

        ii = i + i
        if ii < m:
            arr[ii] += q2

        jj = j + j
        if jj < m:
            arr[jj] += q2

        ij = i + j
        if i != j and ij < m:
            arr[ij] -= 2.0 * q2

        val = float(arr.max())
        if val < best_val - tol:
            best_val = val
            best_delta = delta

    if best_delta > 0.0:
        p[i] = max(0.0, p[i] - best_delta)
        p[j] = p[j] + best_delta
        c += best_delta * d
        q2 = best_delta * best_delta

        ii = i + i
        if ii < m:
            c[ii] += q2

        jj = j + j
        if jj < m:
            c[jj] += q2

        ij = i + j
        if i != j and ij < m:
            c[ij] -= 2.0 * q2

        return float(best_val)

    return None


def power_transform(p, gamma):
    p = np.maximum(np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64), 1e-300)
    return normalize_sum(np.power(p, float(gamma)))


def shape_search(p):
    """
    Cheap global shape changes: reversal, exponentiation, and log-linear
    interpolation between p and reversed(p).
    """
    p = normalize_sum(p)
    n = len(p)

    c = conv_self(p)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_s = score_from_c(c, n)

    bases = [p]
    if n > 1:
        rev = p[::-1].copy()
        c = conv_self(rev)
        s = score_from_c(c, n)
        if s < best_s - 1e-15:
            best_s = s
            best_p = rev.copy()
        bases.append(rev)

    gammas = (0.6, 0.8, 1.0, 1.25, 1.6)
    for base in bases:
        for g in gammas:
            q = power_transform(base, g)
            s = sequence_score_sum1(q)
            if s < best_s - 1e-15:
                best_s = s
                best_p = q.copy()

    if n > 1:
        lp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
        lr = np.log(np.maximum(p[::-1], 1e-300))
        for th in (0.3, 0.5, 0.7):
            q = normalize_sum(np.exp(th * lp + (1.0 - th) * lr))
            s = sequence_score_sum1(q)
            if s < best_s - 1e-15:
                best_s = s
                best_p = q.copy()

    return best_p, float(best_s)


def upscale_sequence(a, n):
    a = normalize_sum(a)
    if len(a) == n:
        return a
    old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, len(a))
    new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    return normalize_sum(np.interp(new, old, a))


def downsample_sequence(a, n_out):
    a = normalize_sum(a)
    if len(a) == n_out:
        return a
    if n_out <= 0:
        return normalize_sum(a)
    ratio = max(1, len(a) // n_out)
    fitted = len(a) - (len(a) % ratio)
    small = a[:fitted].reshape(n_out, ratio).mean(axis=1)
    if len(small) < n_out:
        pad = np.full(n_out - len(small), float(small[-1]) if len(small) else 1.0)
        small = np.concatenate([small, pad])
    return normalize_sum(small[:n_out])


def make_initials(n, given=None):
    inits = []

    if given is not None and len(given) > 0:
        glen = len(given)
        if glen == n:
            inits.append(normalize_sum(given))
        elif glen > n:
            inits.append(downsample_sequence(given, n))
        else:
            inits.append(upscale_sequence(given, n))

    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)

    for a in (0.3, 0.8, 1.6, 3.0):
        p = 1.0 / np.sqrt(idx + a)
        inits.append(normalize_sum(p))
        inits.append(normalize_sum(p[::-1]))
        q = 1.0 / np.sqrt(idx + a) + 1.0 / np.sqrt((n - 1 - idx) + a)
        inits.append(normalize_sum(q))

    if n > 1:
        t = idx / float(n - 1)
        for amp in (0.25, 0.5):
            p = 1.0 + amp * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * t)
            inits.append(normalize_sum(np.maximum(p, 0.1)))

    return inits


def coordinate_local(p, max_time, rng, label=""):
    if max_time <= 0.0:
        p = normalize_sum(p)
        return p, sequence_score_sum1(p)

    p = normalize_sum(p)
    n = len(p)
    c = conv_self(p)
    score = score_from_c(c, n)

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = score

    start = time.monotonic()
    beta = 25.0
    candidates = []
    attempts_since_grad = 10 ** 9
    recompute_count = 0
    since_success = 0

    while time.monotonic() - start < max_time:
        if len(candidates) == 0 or attempts_since_grad >= 25 or since_success > 250:
            w = lse_weights(c, beta)
            g = grad_weights(w, p)

            cand = []

            topg = topk_indices(g, 12, ascending=False)
            botg = topk_indices(g, 12, ascending=True)

            for i in topg[:6]:
                i = int(i)
                if p[i] <= 1e-300:
                    continue
                for j in botg[:6]:
                    j = int(j)
                    if i != j:
                        cand.append((i, j))

            topc = topk_indices(c, 5, ascending=False)
            for kk in topc[:3]:
                k = int(kk)
                gp = peak_gradient_single(p, k)
                if float(gp.max()) <= float(gp.min()) + 1e-300:
                    continue
                topk = topk_indices(gp, 8, ascending=False)
                botk = topk_indices(gp, 8, ascending=True)
                for i in topk[:4]:
                    i = int(i)
                    if p[i] <= 1e-300:
                        continue
                    for j in botk[:4]:
                        j = int(j)
                        if i != j:
                            cand.append((i, j))

            uniq = []
            seen = set()
            for i, j in cand:
                if i == j or i < 0 or j < 0 or i >= n or j >= n:
                    continue
                if p[i] <= 1e-12:
                    continue
                key = (i, j)
                if key in seen:
                    continue
                seen.add(key)
                benefit = float(g[i] - g[j])
                uniq.append((benefit, i, j))

            uniq.sort(reverse=True)
            candidates = [(ii, jj) for (_, ii, jj) in uniq[:30]]

            attempts_since_grad = 0
            since_success = 0

        if recompute_count >= 300:
            c = conv_self(p)
            score = score_from_c(c, n)
            recompute_count = 0
            if score < best_score - 1e-15:
                best_score = score
                best_p = p.copy()

        progress = min(1.0, (time.monotonic() - start) / max(1.0, max_time))
        beta = min(500.0, 20.0 + 480.0 * progress)

        improved = False
        tested = 0

        for i, j in candidates:
            if i == j or p[i] <= 1e-300:
                continue
            tested += 1
            new_score = pair_move(p, c, i, j, PAIR_FRACS)
            if new_score is not None:
                score = new_score
                recompute_count += 1
                if score < best_score - 1e-15:
                    best_score = score
                    best_p = p.copy()
                improved = True
                break

        attempts_since_grad += tested

        if not improved:
            since_success += 1

            # Multi-coordinate escape step, if stuck.
            if since_success > 350 and time.monotonic() - start < max_time - 10.0:
                w = lse_weights(c, beta)
                g = grad_weights(w, p)
                gc = g - g.mean()
                grange = float(g.max() - g.min())
                if grange > 1e-300:
                    base_step = 0.02 / grange
                    accepted = False
                    last_s = np.inf
                    p_try = p
                    c_try = c
                    for factor in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125):
                        p_try = p - factor * base_step * gc
                        p_try = normalize_sum(p_try)
                        c_try = conv_self(p_try)
                        s_try = score_from_c(c_try, n)
                        last_s = s_try
                        if s_try < score - 1e-13:
                            accepted = True
                            break
                    if not accepted and last_s < score and last_s < best_score + 0.01:
                        accepted = True

                    if accepted:
                        p = p_try
                        c = c_try
                        score = float(last_s)
                        if score < best_score - 1e-15:
                            best_score = score
                            best_p = p.copy()
                        since_success = 0
                        attempts_since_grad = 10 ** 9
                        candidates = []
                        continue

            # Random perturbation / restart.
            if since_success > 500:
                noise = (0.004 + 0.012 * progress) / np.sqrt(max(1, n))
                p2 = p + rng.normal(0.0, noise, n)
                p2 = normalize_sum(p2)
                c2 = conv_self(p2)
                s2 = score_from_c(c2, n)

                if s2 <= best_score + 0.02:
                    p = p2
                    c = c2
                    score = s2
                else:
                    p = best_p.copy()
                    c = conv_self(p)
                    score = best_score

                candidates = []
                attempts_since_grad = 10 ** 9
                since_success = 0
                beta = max(10.0, beta * 0.75)

    p = normalize_sum(p)
    c = conv_self(p)
    score = score_from_c(c, n)
    if score < best_score:
        best_p = p.copy()
        best_score = score

    best_p = normalize_sum(best_p)
    return best_p, float(best_score)


def load_given_sequence():
    try:
        raw = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
        if raw is None:
            return None
        arr = np.asarray(raw, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    except Exception:
        return None

    if arr.size == 0:
        return None
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, None)
    if float(arr.sum()) <= 0.0:
        return None
    return arr


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400.0):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start_all = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start_all + max(120.0, float(budget_s)) - 45.0

    given = load_given_sequence()

    if given is not None:
        glen = len(given)
        if 1024 <= glen <= MAX_RETURN_N:
            main_n = glen if glen % 1 == 0 else 8192
        elif glen > MAX_RETURN_N:
            main_n = MAX_RETURN_N
        else:
            main_n = 4096
        # Keep lengths simple for FFT and evaluator.
        if main_n not in (1024, 2048, 4096, 8192, 16384):
            main_n = 8192
    else:
        main_n = 8192

    main_n = int(min(MAX_RETURN_N, max(512, main_n)))

    inits = make_initials(main_n, given)
    best_p = None
    best_score = np.inf

    for init in inits[:14]:
        p = normalize_sum(init)
        c = conv_self(p)
        s = score_from_c(c, len(p))
        if s < best_score:
            best_score = s
            best_p = p.copy()

    if best_p is None:
        best_p = normalize_sum(np.ones(main_n, dtype=np.float64))
        best_score = sequence_score_sum1(best_p)

    print("Initial best: n=%d internal_score=%.8f target=%.4f" % (len(best_p), best_score, TARGET))

    # Initial shape search.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 30.0:
        p_sh, s_sh = shape_search(best_p)
        if s_sh < best_score - 1e-15:
            best_score = s_sh
            best_p = p_sh.copy()
            print("After shape search: %.8f" % best_score)

    # Main exact-coordinate local optimization.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 60.0:
        t1 = min(remaining * 0.55, 900.0)
        p1, s1 = coordinate_local(best_p, t1, rng, label="main1")
        if s1 < best_score - 1e-15:
            best_score = s1
            best_p = p1.copy()
            print("After coordinate local 1: %.8f" % best_score)

    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 30.0:
        p_sh, s_sh = shape_search(best_p)
        if s_sh < best_score - 1e-15:
            best_score = s_sh
            best_p = p_sh.copy()
            print("After shape search 2: %.8f" % best_score)

    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 60.0:
        t2 = min(remaining * 0.55, 700.0)
        p2, s2 = coordinate_local(best_p, t2, rng, label="main2")
        if s2 < best_score - 1e-15:
            best_score = s2
            best_p = p2.copy()
            print("After coordinate local 2: %.8f" % best_score)

    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 30.0:
        p_sh, s_sh = shape_search(best_p)
        if s_sh < best_score - 1e-15:
            best_score = s_sh
            best_p = p_sh.copy()
            print("After shape search 3: %.8f" % best_score)

    # Optional longer sequence attempt, capped at 16384 for evaluator speed.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 500.0 and len(best_p) < 16384:
        if len(best_p) <= 2048:
            large_n = 4096
        elif len(best_p) <= 4096:
            large_n = 8192
        elif len(best_p) <= 8192:
            large_n = 16384
        else:
            large_n = 16384

        if large_n > MAX_RETURN_N:
            large_n = MAX_RETURN_N

        print("\nTrying larger length n=%d with %.0fs remaining" % (large_n, remaining))

        large_inits = []
        large_inits.append(upscale_sequence(best_p, large_n))
        if given is not None:
            large_inits.append(downsample_sequence(given, large_n) if len(given) > large_n else upscale_sequence(given, large_n))

        best_large = None
        best_large_score = np.inf
        for init in large_inits[:4]:
            p = normalize_sum(init)
            s = sequence_score_sum1(p)
            if s < best_large_score:
                best_large_score = s
                best_large = p.copy()

        if best_large is not None:
            if best_large_score < best_score:
                print("  larger initial already better: %.8f" % best_large_score)
            else:
                print("  larger initial not better: %.8f vs %.8f" % (best_large_score, best_score))

            p_sh, s_sh = shape_search(best_large)
            if s_sh < best_large_score:
                best_large_score = s_sh
                best_large = p_sh.copy()

            large_time = min((deadline - time.monotonic()) * 0.75, 800.0)
            if large_time > 60.0:
                p3, s3 = coordinate_local(best_large, large_time, rng, label="large")
                if s3 < best_large_score:
                    best_large_score = s3
                    best_large = p3.copy()

            if best_large is not None and best_large_score < best_score - 1e-9:
                best_score = best_large_score
                best_p = best_large.copy()
                print("  accepted larger n=%d score=%.8f" % (len(best_p), best_score))
            else:
                print("  keeping previous n=%d score=%.8f" % (len(best_p), best_score))

    # Final short polish if time remains.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 45.0:
        p_final_pol, s_final_pol = coordinate_local(best_p, min(remaining - 25.0, 400.0), rng, label="final")
        if s_final_pol < best_score - 1e-15:
            best_score = s_final_pol
            best_p = p_final_pol.copy()
            print("After final polish: %.8f" % best_score)

    best_p = normalize_sum(best_p)
    best_p = np.clip(best_p, 0.0, 1000.0)

    if len(best_p) == 0 or float(best_p.sum()) <= 1e-300:
        best_p = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64) / 1024.0

    final_list = [float(x) for x in best_p]

    try:
        ver = evaluate_sequence(final_list)
        print("Final evaluate_sequence check: %r" % ver)
        if not np.isfinite(ver):
            # Extremely defensive fallback.
            best_p = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64) / 1024.0
            final_list = [float(x) for x in best_p]
    except Exception as e:
        print("Final evaluate_sequence check failed: %s" % e)

    print("Returning n=%d, best internal score=%.8f, target=%.4f" % (len(best_p), best_score, TARGET))
    return final_list
```
